People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Um… I guess the main thing that finally got me to come in is my eating has just gotten really strange. Like, some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers and sweets, like I didn’t even plan to.

I kept thinking it was just stress or maybe I needed to buy better groceries—because if there are crackers in the house, I mean, who doesn’t eat them—but it’s been all over the place enough that it started bothering me.

What I noticed more is my appetite being strange… like what finally pushed me to come in is that my eating has been all over the place. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\nSo weight-wise, I’m not sure how significant it’s been… I just know the eating pattern doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over your eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe not fully in control, but I don’t know if I’d call it that exactly. It’s more like the whole eating thing has been all over the place, and that’s what got me worried enough to come in.\n\nSome weeks I barely want dinner at all, and then other nights I’m just standing there in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed, eating crackers or something sweet. And then I’ll sort of snap back and think, why am I even doing this? So… yeah, it doesn’t feel very normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do these late-night eating episodes happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Regularly… I mean, not like every single night on a schedule or anything. It’s more uneven than that.\n\nSome weeks I barely even touch dinner, and then other nights I’ll end up in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets. That’s kind of what finally made me think, okay, this isn’t just normal snacking. It’s just been all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight when you're eating at those late-night times?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s exactly gaining weight I’m worried about. I mean, I don’t love the idea, but in those moments I’m not really thinking clearly like, “Oh no, calories,” or anything.\n\nIt’s more afterward I get bothered, like… why was I doing that at midnight? And then I start thinking about how my clothes fit, or remembering when my sister used to comment on people’s weight at family dinners—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. I guess the short answer is I’m not sure. It’s not always about weight in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating because you're really hungry during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, but not always. That’s the thing that feels strange to me.\n\nLike, some weeks I barely touch dinner, so maybe later I am hungry, I don’t know. But then other nights I’ll be standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or something sweet, and it doesn’t really feel like normal hunger exactly. It’s more like I’m just doing it, and then afterward I think, why did I do that?\n\nThat back-and-forth is really what pushed me to come in.\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen in a typical month?\n\nPatient: I’m not great at counting it, honestly. In a typical month… maybe it comes in patches? Like there’ll be a week where I barely eat dinner, and then a few nights where I’m in the kitchen late eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s asleep.\n\nSo it’s not every night, but it happens enough that I noticed it and it bothered me. That’s really what pushed me to come in—my eating just feels all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean… sometimes it feels that way, but I’m not sure if I’d call it completely unable to stop. What really got me worried is that my eating has just been all over the place.\n\nSome weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or something sweet, and it feels kind of automatic. Like I’ll realize I’m doing it and feel embarrassed afterward. I used to keep crackers in this one cabinet near the sink—sorry, that’s not really important—but yeah, it’s that swing between hardly eating and then late-night snacking that finally made me think I should talk to someone.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah… I think sometimes I do. I mean, not always on purpose like, “I’m restricting,” but some weeks I’ll barely touch dinner, or I’ll just sort of skip over eating much because I don’t feel like it.

And then later, after everyone’s gone to bed, I’ll be in the kitchen eating crackers or something sweet. It’s embarrassing. I used to do that when the house was quiet—well, I still do sometimes—and I don’t know if it’s because I didn’t eat enough earlier or if it’s just this weird pattern I’ve gotten into.

That’s really what pushed me to come in, honestly. My eating has just been all over the place.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess the main thing that finally got me to come in is my eating has just gotten really strange. Like, some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers and sweets, like I didn’t even plan to.\n\nI kept thinking it was just stress or maybe I needed to buy better groceries—because if there are crackers in the house, I mean, who doesn’t eat them—but it’s been all over the place enough that it started bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're eating very quickly or eating a lot of food in a short amount of time during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, sometimes. It’s not like I sit down with a plate exactly, it’s more like I’m just standing there in the kitchen, kind of picking at crackers or something sweet, and then suddenly I realize I’ve had a bunch. It’s weird because other weeks I’m barely eating dinner at all, and then I’ll have these nights after everyone’s asleep where it’s like I’m just… eating without really meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Not always in a simple way, like “oh I ate crackers, that’s bad,” but I’ll start feeling like I messed everything up somehow.\n\nI can sit there afterward and my brain starts replaying little things—like if I bought an expensive tube of paint pigment that week, or if I snapped at my spouse earlier—and then suddenly it feels like I’ve ruined the whole household. Like the eating is just one more proof that I can’t keep things together. It gets kind of… bigger than it probably should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, I don’t think that’s part of it for me. I don’t go and, like, plan something afterward. Mostly I just feel embarrassed and start cleaning up the crumbs or wrappers, and then I get stuck thinking about why I did it. But vomiting or laxatives… no, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you usually eat like this when you're feeling stressed or emotional?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah… stress probably has something to do with it. It’s just hard to pin down because my eating has been kind of all over the place lately. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights, after everyone’s gone to bed, I’m standing in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets like I didn’t even decide to do it.\n\nAnd then I’ll think, well, maybe I was upset that day, or maybe I was just tired… but it all kind of runs together. That’s actually part of what finally made me come in, because it’s been so inconsistent and I don’t feel like I have a normal rhythm with food right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at those late-night times helps you feel better emotionally, even if it's just for a little while?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “better” is quite the word… maybe distracted for a few minutes? But honestly what stands out more is how uneven it’s gotten.\n\nLike, some weeks I’ll barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets, sort of like I wandered into it. That up-and-down is really what pushed me to come in, because it doesn’t feel like a normal pattern anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really tracked the number on the scale, honestly. What I noticed more is my appetite being strange… like what finally pushed me to come in is that my eating has been all over the place. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\nSo weight-wise, I’m not sure how significant it’s been… I just know the eating pattern doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over your eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe not fully in control, but I don’t know if I’d call it that exactly. It’s more like the whole eating thing has been all over the place, and that’s what got me worried enough to come in.\n\nSome weeks I barely want dinner at all, and then other nights I’m just standing there in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed, eating crackers or something sweet. And then I’ll sort of snap back and think, why am I even doing this? So… yeah, it doesn’t feel very normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do these late-night eating episodes happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Regularly… I mean, not like every single night on a schedule or anything. It’s more uneven than that.\n\nSome weeks I barely even touch dinner, and then other nights I’ll end up in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets. That’s kind of what finally made me think, okay, this isn’t just normal snacking. It’s just been all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight when you're eating at those late-night times?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s exactly gaining weight I’m worried about. I mean, I don’t love the idea, but in those moments I’m not really thinking clearly like, “Oh no, calories,” or anything.\n\nIt’s more afterward I get bothered, like… why was I doing that at midnight? And then I start thinking about how my clothes fit, or remembering when my sister used to comment on people’s weight at family dinners—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. I guess the short answer is I’m not sure. It’s not always about weight in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating because you're really hungry during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, but not always. That’s the thing that feels strange to me.\n\nLike, some weeks I barely touch dinner, so maybe later I am hungry, I don’t know. But then other nights I’ll be standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or something sweet, and it doesn’t really feel like normal hunger exactly. It’s more like I’m just doing it, and then afterward I think, why did I do that?\n\nThat back-and-forth is really what pushed me to come in.\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen in a typical month?\n\nPatient: I’m not great at counting it, honestly. In a typical month… maybe it comes in patches? Like there’ll be a week where I barely eat dinner, and then a few nights where I’m in the kitchen late eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s asleep.\n\nSo it’s not every night, but it happens enough that I noticed it and it bothered me. That’s really what pushed me to come in—my eating just feels all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean… sometimes it feels that way, but I’m not sure if I’d call it completely unable to stop. What really got me worried is that my eating has just been all over the place.\n\nSome weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or something sweet, and it feels kind of automatic. Like I’ll realize I’m doing it and feel embarrassed afterward. I used to keep crackers in this one cabinet near the sink—sorry, that’s not really important—but yeah, it’s that swing between hardly eating and then late-night snacking that finally made me think I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you often skip meals or restrict your food intake during the day before these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think sometimes I do. I don’t know… I haven’t really thought of it that way. I mean, I notice my clothes sometimes and then I get irritated, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting there thinking, “I can’t gain weight,” exactly.

Not really like… I’m worried about my weight, no. I mean, I notice it sometimes, but that’s not what I’m thinking during the day.

It’s not like I sit down with a plate exactly, it’s more like I’m just standing there in the kitchen, kind of picking at crackers or something sweet, and then suddenly I realize I’ve had a bunch. It’s weird because other weeks I’m barely eating dinner at all, and then I’ll have these nights after everyone’s asleep where it’s like I’m just… eating without really meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Not always in a simple way, like “oh I ate crackers, that’s bad,” but I’ll start feeling like I messed everything up somehow.\n\nI can sit there afterward and my brain starts replaying little things—like if I bought an expensive tube of paint pigment that week, or if I snapped at my spouse earlier—and then suddenly it feels like I’ve ruined the whole household. Like the eating is just one more proof that I can’t keep things together. It gets kind of… bigger than it probably should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, I don’t think that’s part of it for me. I don’t go and, like, plan something afterward. Mostly I just feel embarrassed and start cleaning up the crumbs or wrappers, and then I get stuck thinking about why I did it. But vomiting or laxatives… no, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you usually eat like this when you're feeling stressed or emotional?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah… stress probably has something to do with it. It’s just hard to pin down because my eating has been kind of all over the place lately. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights, after everyone’s gone to bed, I’m standing in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets like I didn’t even decide to do it.\n\nAnd then I’ll think, well, maybe I was upset that day, or maybe I was just tired… but it all kind of runs together. That’s actually part of what finally made me come in, because it’s been so inconsistent and I don’t feel like I have a normal rhythm with food right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at those late-night times helps you feel better emotionally, even if it's just for a little while?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “better” is quite the word… maybe distracted for a few minutes? But honestly what stands out more is how uneven it’s gotten.\n\nLike, some weeks I’ll barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets, sort of like I wandered into it. That up-and-down is really what pushed me to come in, because it doesn’t feel like a normal pattern anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really tracked the number on the scale, honestly. What I noticed more is my appetite being strange… like what finally pushed me to come in is that my eating has been all over the place. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\nSo weight-wise, I’m not sure how significant it’s been… I just know the eating pattern doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over your eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe not fully in control, but I don’t know if I’d call it that exactly. It’s more like the whole eating thing has been all over the place, and that’s what got me worried enough to come in.\n\nSome weeks I barely want dinner at all, and then other nights I’m just standing there in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed, eating crackers or something sweet. And then I’ll sort of snap back and think, why am I even doing this? So… yeah, it doesn’t feel very normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do these late-night eating episodes happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Regularly… I mean, not like every single night on a schedule or anything. It’s more uneven than that.\n\nSome weeks I barely even touch dinner, and then other nights I’ll end up in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets. That’s kind of what finally made me think, okay, this isn’t just normal snacking. It’s just been all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight when you're eating at those late-night times?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s exactly gaining weight I’m worried about. I mean, I don’t love the idea, but in those moments I’m not really thinking clearly like, “Oh no, calories,” or anything.\n\nIt’s more afterward I get bothered, like… why was I doing that at midnight? And then I start thinking about how my clothes fit, or remembering when my sister used to comment on people’s weight at family dinners—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. I guess the short answer is I’m not sure. It’s not always about weight in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating because you're really hungry during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, but not always. That’s the thing that feels strange to me.\n\nLike, some weeks I barely touch dinner, so maybe later I am hungry, I don’t know. But then other nights I’ll be standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or something sweet, and it doesn’t really feel like normal hunger exactly. It’s more like I’m just doing it, and then afterward I think, why did I do that?\n\nThat back-and-forth is really what pushed me to come in.\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen in a typical month?\n\nPatient: I’m not great at counting it, honestly. In a typical month… maybe it comes in patches? Like there’ll be a week where I barely eat dinner, and then a few nights where I’m in the kitchen late eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s asleep.\n\nSo it’s not every night, but it happens enough that I noticed it and it bothered me. That’s really what pushed me to come in—my eating just feels all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean… sometimes it feels that way, but I’m not sure if I’d call it completely unable to stop. My eating has just been all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are influenced by a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really thought of it that way. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think it’s really about wanting to lose weight, or avoiding gaining weight… not in a clear way, anyway.

What finally pushed me to come in is just that my eating is all over the place. Like some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers and sweets, and I’ll be thinking about how I forgot to put the laundry in the dryer or whatever, and then suddenly I’ve eaten a bunch. So… I don’t know. It feels more uneven than weight-focused.

It’s not like I sit down with a plate exactly, it’s more like I’m just standing there in the kitchen, kind of picking at crackers or something sweet, and then suddenly I realize I’ve had a bunch. It’s weird because other weeks I’m barely eating dinner at all, and then I’ll have these nights after everyone’s asleep where it’s like I’m just… eating without really meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Not always in a simple way, like “oh I ate crackers, that’s bad,” but I’ll start feeling like I messed everything up somehow.\n\nI can sit there afterward and my brain starts replaying little things—like if I bought an expensive tube of paint pigment that week, or if I snapped at my spouse earlier—and then suddenly it feels like I’ve ruined the whole household. Like the eating is just one more proof that I can’t keep things together. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights, after everyone’s gone to bed, I’m standing in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets like I didn’t even decide to do it.\n\nAnd then I’ll think, well, maybe I was upset that day, or maybe I was just tired… but it all kind of runs together. That’s actually part of what finally made me come in, because it’s been so inconsistent and I don’t feel like I have a normal rhythm with food right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at those late-night times helps you feel better emotionally, even if it's just for a little while?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “better” is quite the word… maybe distracted for a few minutes? That up-and-down is really what pushed me to come in, because it doesn’t feel like a normal pattern anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really tracked the number on the scale, honestly. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\nSo weight-wise, I’m not sure how significant it’s been… I just know the eating pattern doesn’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over your eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe not fully in control, but I don’t know if I’d call it that exactly. It’s more like the whole eating thing has been all over the place, and that’s what got me worried enough to come in.\n\nSome weeks I barely want dinner at all, and then other nights I’m just standing there in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed, eating crackers or something sweet. And then I’ll sort of snap back and think, why am I even doing this? It’s more uneven than that.\n\nSome weeks I barely even touch dinner, and then other nights I’ll end up in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets. That’s kind of what finally made me think, okay, this isn’t just normal snacking. It’s just been all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight when you're eating at those late-night times?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s exactly gaining weight I’m worried about. I mean, I don’t love the idea, but in those moments I’m not really thinking clearly like, “Oh no, calories,” or anything.\n\nIt’s more afterward I get bothered, like… why was I doing that at midnight? And then I start thinking about how my clothes fit, or remembering when my sister used to comment on people’s weight at family dinners—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. I guess the short answer is I’m not sure. It’s not always about weight in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating because you're really hungry during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, but not always. That’s the thing that feels strange to me.\n\nLike, some weeks I barely touch dinner, so maybe later I am hungry, I don’t know. But then other nights I’ll be standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or something sweet, and it doesn’t really feel like normal hunger exactly. It’s more like I’m just doing it, and then afterward I think, why did I do that?\n\nThat back-and-forth is really what pushed me to come in.\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen in a typical month?\n\nPatient: I’m not great at counting it, honestly. In a typical month… maybe it comes in patches? Like there’ll be a week where I barely eat dinner, and then a few nights where I’m in the kitchen late eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s asleep.\n\nSo it’s not every night, but it happens enough that I noticed it and it bothered me. That’s really what pushed me to come in—my eating just feels all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean… sometimes it feels that way, but I’m not sure if I’d call it completely unable to stop. What really got me worried is that my eating has just been all over the place.\n\nSome weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or something sweet, and it feels kind of automatic. Like I’ll realize I’m doing it and feel embarrassed afterward. I used to keep crackers in this one cabinet near the sink—sorry, that’s not really important—but yeah, it’s that swing between hardly eating and then late-night snacking that finally made me think I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you often skip meals or restrict your food intake during the day before these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think sometimes I do. I mean, not always on purpose like, “I’m restricting,” but some weeks I’ll barely touch dinner, or I’ll just sort of skip over eating much because I don’t feel like it.\n\nAnd then later, after everyone’s gone to bed, I’ll be in the kitchen eating crackers or something sweet. It’s embarrassing. I used to do that when the house was quiet—well, I still do sometimes—and I don’t know if it’s because I didn’t eat enough earlier or if it’s just this weird pattern I’ve gotten into.\n\nThat’s really what pushed me to come in, honestly. My eating has just been all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are influenced by a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really thought of it that way. I mean, I notice my clothes sometimes and then I get irritated, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting there thinking, “I can’t gain weight,” exactly.\n\nIt feels more like my eating just gets weird depending on the day. It’s more like I’m just doing it, and then afterward I think, why did I do that?\n\nThat back-and-forth is really what pushed me to come in.\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen in a typical month?\n\nPatient: I’m not great at counting it, honestly. In a typical month… maybe it comes in patches? Like there’ll be a week where I barely eat dinner, and then a few nights where I’m in the kitchen late eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s asleep.\n\nSo it’s not every night, but it happens enough that I noticed it and it bothered me. That’s really what pushed me to come in—my eating just feels all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean… sometimes it feels that way, but I’m not sure if I’d call it completely unable to stop. My eating has just been all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are influenced by a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really thought of it that way. What really got me worried is that my eating has just been all over the place.\n\nSome weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or something sweet, and it feels kind of automatic. Like I’ll realize I’m doing it and feel embarrassed afterward. I used to keep crackers in this one cabinet near the sink—sorry, that’s not really important—but yeah, it’s that swing between hardly eating and then late-night snacking that finally made me think I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Do you often skip meals or restrict your food intake during the day before these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think sometimes I do. I mean, not always on purpose like, “I’m restricting,” but some weeks I’ll barely touch dinner, or I’ll just sort of skip over eating much because I don’t feel like it.\n\nAnd then later, after everyone’s gone to bed, I’ll be in the kitchen eating crackers or something sweet. It’s embarrassing. I used to do that when the house was quiet—well, I still do sometimes—and I don’t know if it’s because I didn’t eat enough earlier or if it’s just this weird pattern I’ve gotten into.\n\nThat’s really what pushed me to come in, honestly. My eating has just been all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are influenced by a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really thought of it that way. I mean, I notice my clothes sometimes and then I get irritated, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting there thinking, “I can’t gain weight,” exactly.\n\nIt feels more like my eating just gets weird depending on the day. Like I’ll mean to make something normal, and then I get distracted by dishes or mail on the counter—there’s always mail piled up—and then suddenly it’s late and I’m eating whatever’s easy. So… maybe not fear of gaining weight? I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you often try to limit how much you eat during the day because you're worried about your weight?\n\nPatient: Not really like… I’m worried about my weight, no. I mean, I notice it sometimes, but that’s not what I’m thinking during the day.\n\nIt’s more that my eating has just been really uneven, and that’s what made me come in. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets, just standing there like… I don’t even know why I’m doing it. And then I feel embarrassed. So I don’t know if I’m limiting it on purpose during the day, exactly. It just happens that way sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often try to eat as little as possible during the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m trying to eat as little as possible, exactly. It’s more like… it just gets all over the place.\n\nSome weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’ll end up standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or something sweet. And then I feel stupid about it, like why didn’t I just eat normally earlier? That’s kind of what finally made me come in, because it’s been so uneven and I can’t seem to get it straight.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating patterns are affected by wanting to lose weight or avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s really about wanting to lose weight, or avoiding gaining weight… not in a clear way, anyway.\n\nWhat finally pushed me to come in is just that my eating is all over the place. Like some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers and sweets, and I’ll be thinking about how I forgot to put the laundry in the dryer or whatever, and then suddenly I’ve eaten a bunch. So… I don’t know. It feels more uneven than weight-focused.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you often restrict your food intake on purpose during the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m doing it on purpose, not like making a rule for myself or anything. It’s more… my eating has just been really all over the place.\n\nSome weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed, eating crackers or sweets, just kind of standing there.
